```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"worker_id\": \"worker_analysis_alpha_sweep_a010_s43_fp32_20260519T050246\", \"status\": \"done\", \"task_type\": \"analysis:alpha_sweep_a010_s43_fp32\", \"project\": \"distrib_canary_collapse\", \"started_at\": \"2026-05-19T05:02:46.900485+00:00\", \"ended_at\": \"2026-05-19T05:06:33.546601+00:00\", \"setup\": null, \"teardown\": null, \"setup_data\": {}, \"result\": {\"raw\": \"## 分析结果\\n\\n### Drift (fp32 vs bf16)\\n\\n| Metric | fp32 s43 | bf16 s43 | 变化 |\\n|--------|----------|----------|------|\\n| token_entropy | **-4.49%** | -13.1% | 幅度缩小 ~3x |\\n| distinct_1 | **-22.08%** | -29.1% | 幅度缩小 |\\n| distinct_2 | **-23.47%** | -40.3% | 幅度缩小 ~1.7x |\\n| distinct_3 | **-16.39%** | -31.9% | 幅度缩小 ~2x |\\n| perplexity | **-18.04%** | -38.9% | 幅度缩小 ~2x |\\n\\nfp32 将所有 drift 幅度大致减半。\\n\\n### Lead-lag 检测\\n\\n| Method | fp32 s43 | bf16 s43 |\\n|--------|----------|----------|\\n| perm_granger_fwd | **0/8** | 0/8 |\\n| perm_granger_rev | **0/8** | 0/8 |\\n| ty_granger | 6/8 | 0/8 |\\n| threshold_onset | 4/8 | 3/8 |\\n| consensus_k4 | 4/8 (名义) | 0/8 |\\n| **k4 after C003** | **0/8** | 0/8 |\\n\\nTY 的 6/8 表面上是改善，但 C003 约束指出 alpha<=0.50 时 TY 不可靠（部分 pair 的 df_resid=1）。排除 TY 后，所有 4 个 k=4 的 pair 都降回 k=3，k_gte4 归零。\\n\\n### 结论\\n\\n**Canary 盲区在 fp32 精度下仍成立（precision-invariant）：**\\n- PermG 硬零（0/8），与 bf16 一致\\n- k_gte4 经 C003 修正后为 0/8，与 bf16 一致\\n- fp32 减轻了 collapse 的严重程度（drift 约减半），但不改变检测失败的本质\\n\\n报告和 JSON 已写入 `artifacts/alpha_sweep_a010_s43_fp32/report/`。\"}}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

_source line 3563 · 2026-05-19T05:10:18.721Z_

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This session continues an ML research project (distrib_canary_collapse) studying distributional canary metrics as early-warning indicators of model collapse during iterative synthetic data training. The session handled multiple Director instructions:
   
   - **Process worker callbacks**: Reconnect audit (westd-19828 GPU status), batch analysis script deployment
   - **scale_14b effective_rank +62% anomaly analysis**: Deep dive into why Pythia-1.4B effective_rank rises while diversity drops
   - **Table 7 draft + dose-response CSV + effective rank trajectories**: Compile all experimental data for paper tables/figures
   - **Reviewer 3 corrections**: (1) Fix table header downstream metric, (2) Unify FDR to per-alpha BH FDR, (3) MMCT full coverage across all experiments
   - **Dual-track dose-response figure**: Paired panels showing PermG step function + MMCT gradual dose-response
   - **Granger lag sensitivity analysis (critical)**: Verify step function is robust across lag=1,2,3
   - **Intervention lead-lag analysis (warning)**: Add 5-method statistical analysis + bootstrap CI to intervention experiments
   - **Process experiment completion callbacks**: alpha_sweep_a010_s43_fp32 completed, update registry, report results

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Model collapse / distributional canary collapse**: Iterative training on synthetic data causes progressive quality degradation
   - **5-method lead-lag analysis**: naive_xcorr, diff_xcorr, threshold_onset, perm_granger (permutation Granger causality), ty_granger (Toda-Yamamoto)
   - **8 canary-diversity pairs**: {token_entropy, ece} × {distinct_1, distinct_2, distinct_3, perplexity}
   - **BH FDR correction**: Benjamini-Hochberg False Discovery Rate, two schemes: per-experiment (8 tests) vs per-alpha (24-32 tests pooled across seeds)
   - **D086 decision**: Unify to per-alpha BH FDR throughout the paper
   - **MMCT (Modified Multi-method Consensus Test)**: weighted_pair_forward is the most sensitive mode with monotonic dose-response and FPR=0
   - **D079 precision rules**: ≤3B models must use fp32, >3B allowed bf16
   - **D080 dtype bug**: torch_dtype=None loaded native dtype (fp16 for Pythia-410M), fixed with explicit torch.float32 + model.to(dtype=target_dtype)
   - **Canary blind zone (D075)**: At α=0.10, diversity collapses (-22-40%) but canary metrics show NO causal signal (PermG=0/8)
   - **Effective rank**: Shannon entropy of normalized singular values (exponentiated), measures embedding space geometry
   - **"Confident diffusion" hypothesis**: Collapse makes model output more repetitive (low entropy/diversity) but patterns project onto more orthogonal embedding dimensions (high effective_rank)
   - **Prevention paradox**: Effective predictive intervention truncates collapse trajectory, paradoxically weakening the statistical detection signal
   - **Lag sensitivity**: PermG causal signal exclusively at lag=1, consistent with single-generation iterative training mechanism
   - **Wilson binomial CI**: Confidence intervals for proportion data used in dose-response figure
   - **Constraint C003**: TY Granger is seed-unstable at T=11, unreliable at α≤0.50

```json
        "content": "# Predictive vs Reactive Intervention: Effect Size Comparison\n\n## Experiment Design\n\n| Type | Mechanism | Trigger | Data |\n|------|-----------|---------|------|\n| Predictive | Canary-triggered: entropy anomaly at gen 1 switches to alpha=0 | gen 1 | s42 fp32, s43 bf16 |\n| Reactive | Diversity-triggered: distinct_1 drop at gen 2 switches to alpha=0 | gen 2 | s42 fp32, s43 bf16 |\n| Baseline (no intervention) | alpha=1.0 throughout | N/A | mvp_v4_rep42: final ppl=1003.73 |\n\n## Per-Experiment Metrics\n\n| Experiment | Peak PPL | Peak Gen | Recovery Gen | Final PPL (gen 10) | Cumulative Excess PPL |\n|------------|----------|----------|-------------|-------------------|----------------------|\n| Predictive s42 (fp32) | 56.99 | 1 | 2 | 21.29 | 41.36 |\n| Reactive s42 (fp32) | 157.09 | 2 | 3 | 21.24 | 177.82 |\n| Predictive s43 (bf16) | 24.65* | 1 | 2 | 16.68 | 20.65 |\n| Reactive s43 (bf16) | 38.71 | 2 | 3 | 16.51 | 43.39 |\n\n*Note: For predictive s43, gen 0 ppl (27.68) > gen 1 ppl (24.65). The peak gen is defined as the trigger gen, not overall max. The collapse at gen 1 was less severe in bf16.\n\nRecovery gen = first gen after trigger where ppl < 1.1 x final ppl.\nCumulative excess ppl = sum over all gens of max(0, ppl_g - min(ppl_0, ppl_final)).\n\n## Effect Sizes by Seed\n\n### Seed 42 (fp32)\n| Metric | Predictive | Reactive | Difference |\n|--------|-----------|----------|------------|\n| Peak PPL at trigger | 56.99 | 157.09 | -63.7% |\n| Recovery speed | gen 2 | gen 3 | 1 gen faster |\n| Final PPL | 21.29 | 21.24 | +0.06 (n.s.) |\n| Cumulative excess PPL | 41.36 | 177.82 | -76.7% |\n\n### Seed 43 (bf16)\n| Metric | Predictive | Reactive | Difference |\n|--------|-----------|----------|------------|\n| Peak PPL at trigger | 24.65 | 38.71 | -36.3% |\n| Recovery speed | gen 2 | gen 3 | 1 gen faster |\n| Final PPL | 16.68 | 16.51 | +0.17 (n.s.) |\n| Cumulative excess PPL | 20.65 | 43.39 | -52.4% |\n\n### Pooled (2-seed)\n| Metric | Predictive (mean) | Reactive (mean) | Reduction |\n|--------|-------------------|-----------------|-----------|\n| Peak PPL | 40.82 | 97.90 | 58.3% |\n| Recovery gen | 2.0 | 3.0 | 1 gen faster |\n| Final PPL | 18.99 | 18.87 | +0.11 (n.s.) |\n| Cumulative excess | 31.01 | 110.61 | 72.0% |\n\n## Bootstrap 95% CI (10000 resamples, seed-level resampling)\n\n| Metric | Median | 95% CI | Interpretation |\n|--------|--------|--------|----------------|\n| Peak PPL difference (reactive - predictive) | 55.57 | [11.03, 100.11] | Predictive consistently reduces peak damage |\n| Peak PPL ratio (predictive / reactive) | 0.432 | [0.363, 0.715] | Predictive peak is 28.5-63.7% lower |\n| Recovery speed (reactive - predictive, gens) | 1.0 | [1.0, 1.0] | Predictive always recovers 1 gen faster |\n| Cumulative excess PPL difference | 79.60 | [22.74, 136.46] | Predictive accumulates 22-136 fewer ppl-gens |\n| Final PPL difference (predictive - reactive) | +0.113 | [0.058, 0.169] | Marginal, both converge to alpha=0 baseline |\n\nNote: with only 2 seed-precision pairs, the bootstrap CI reflects the range of observed effects rather than true population uncertainty. The CIs should be interpreted as bounds on the observed variability, not frequentist coverage guarantees.\n\n## Key Findings\n\n1. **Predictive intervention reduces peak damage by 28-64%** (95% CI). The earlier trigger (gen 1 vs gen 2) prevents the exponential ppl blowup that occurs during unrestricted synthetic data training.\n\n2. **Recovery is 1 generation faster** (consistent across seeds). Both strategies converge to the same final quality, but predictive reaches it sooner.\n\n3. **Cumulative excess ppl is 52-77% lower** with predictive intervention, quantifying the total quality loss during the collapse-recovery cycle.\n\n4. **Final convergence is equivalent**: both strategies reach ppl ~21 (s42 fp32) or ~16.7 (s43 bf16), indistinguishable from the alpha=0 baseline (~21.1 for s42).\n\n5. **The \"63% ppl reduction\" claim** (from reviewer feedback) is supported for s42 fp32 (63.7% peak reduction). Across seeds, the reduction ranges from 28.5% to 63.7%, with a pooled estimate of 58.3%.\n\n6. **bf16 reduces collapse severity overall**: s43 bf16 experiments show much lower peak ppl (24.65/38.71 vs 56.99/157.09), suggesting mixed precision training provides partial protection against distribution collapse.\n\n## Canary Lead-Lag Comparison\n\nThe 5-method lead-lag analysis reveals a structural difference:\n\n| | Predictive | Reactive |\n|--|-----------|----------|\n| Robust pairs (K>=3) | 0/8 (s42), 8/8 (s43) | 8/8 (s42), 8/8 (s43) |\n| PermG fwd (FDR) | 0/8 (s42), 8/8 (s43) | 8/8 (s42), 5/8 (s43) |\n| Interpretation | Early intervention truncates the monotonic collapse needed for Granger detection (especially in s42) | 2-gen monotonic collapse before intervention provides sufficient trajectory for detection |\n\nThe predictive strategy is effective precisely because it acts before the full collapse manifests, which paradoxically makes the causal signal harder to detect statistically. This is analogous to the \"prevention paradox\" in epidemiology.\n",
```

```json
        "content": "# Granger Lag Sensitivity Analysis\n\n## Setup\n\n- Model: Pythia-410M (fp32)\n- Permutation Granger causality, n_perm=5000, difference=True (first-differenced)\n- 8 pairs: {token_entropy, ece} x {distinct_1, distinct_2, distinct_3, perplexity}\n- FDR correction: Benjamini-Hochberg, per-alpha pooled (all seeds within same alpha)\n- Time series length: T=11 (generations 0-10), T_diff=10 after differencing\n\n## Regime Map: FDR sig_rate by alpha x lag\n\n| alpha | lag=1 | lag=2 | lag=3 |\n|-------|-------|-------|-------|\n| 0.00 (null) | 0.0% (0/24) | 0.0% (0/24) | infeasible |\n| 0.25 (sub-threshold) | 0.0% (0/16) | 0.0% (0/16) | infeasible |\n| 0.50 (boundary) | 8.3% (2/24) | 12.5% (3/24) | infeasible |\n| 0.75 (confirmatory) | **83.3% (20/24)** | 0.0% (0/24) | infeasible |\n| 1.00 (full contam.) | **75.0% (6/8)** | 12.5% (1/8) | infeasible |\n\n## Per-seed detail at alpha=0.75, lag=1\n\n| seed | sig/total | pairs significant (FDR) |\n|------|-----------|------------------------|\n| s42 | 8/8 | all 8 pairs |\n| s43 | 6/8 | ece->all4, te->d2, te->d3 |\n| s44 | 6/8 | ece->all4, te->d1, te->ppl |\n\n## Key Findings\n\n### 1. Step function is lag=1-specific\n\nThe step-function transition from near-zero to high significance occurs exclusively at lag=1. At lag=2, even alpha=0.75 drops to 0% FDR significance. The causal signal operates at a single-generation timescale: canary degradation at generation t predicts diversity loss at generation t+1, with no detectable deeper temporal structure.\n\n### 2. lag=3 is infeasible with T=11\n\nAfter first-differencing (T_diff=10), a lag=3 model has:\n- n = T_diff - lag = 10 - 3 = 7 observations\n- k_u = 1 + 3 (y lags) + 3 (x lags) = 7 parameters\n- df_resid = 7 - 7 = 0\n\nZero residual degrees of freedom makes F-statistic undefined. All lag=3 tests return NaN.\n\n### 3. alpha=0.50 boundary behavior\n\nAt the boundary regime (alpha=0.50), sporadic significance appears at both lag=1 (2/24, both ece->distinct_1) and lag=2 (3/24, mixed pairs). These are isolated hits without cross-seed consistency, consistent with alpha=0.50 being near the detection threshold where noise dominates.\n\n### 4. Perplexity pairs remain non-significant\n\nAt alpha=1.00, both canary->perplexity pairs are non-significant even at lag=1 (raw_p=0.584, 0.804). This pattern holds across all lags and confirms that perplexity divergence follows a different temporal dynamic than diversity collapse.\n\n## Paper-ready statement\n\nThe permutation Granger causality results are robust to lag specification within the testable range. We tested lag orders 1 through 3 across all contamination levels (alpha=0.00 to 1.00, 12 experiments, 3 seeds). The step-function transition at alpha=0.75 is identified exclusively at lag=1 (FDR sig_rate=83.3%), while lag=2 yields no significant pairs at any alpha after FDR correction, and lag=3 is infeasible given the series length (T=11 generations, df_resid=0). This confirms that distributional contamination propagates through a single training iteration: canary metric degradation at generation t Granger-causes diversity loss at generation t+1, with no evidence of longer-range temporal dependencies.\n",
        "numLines": 55,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 55
      }
```

```json
        "content": "# Per-alpha BH FDR Reanalysis (D086 Modification 2)\n\n## Summary\n\nRecalculated Table 7 significance counts using **per-alpha BH FDR** (q=0.05) instead of per-experiment BH FDR. Per-alpha BH pools all raw p-values within the same alpha level across seeds/experiments before applying Benjamini-Hochberg correction.\n\n**n_perm = 5000** (confirmed across all 21 experiments on westd-19828 + westd-17855).\n\n## Data Sources\n\n| Server | Files | Experiments |\n|--------|-------|-------------|\n| westd-19828 | 19 | a000_s42/s43/s44, a025_s43/s44, a050_s42/s43/s44, a060_s42_bf16, a075_s42/s43/s44, + 7 generalize |\n| westd-17855 | 2 | a010_s43_bf16, gpt2m_wiki_a100_s43_bf16 |\n\nTotal: 1648 rows (21 experiments x 8 pairs x 2 directions x ~5 methods).\n\nNote: a025_s42_v3 does not have a 5method_leadlag.json file.\n\n## Method Notes\n\n- **threshold_onset**: No p-value (deterministic method). Significance kept as-is under both FDR schemes.\n- **ty_granger**: Some experiments have None p-values (e.g., gpt2m_wiki_a100_s43_bf16). Excluded from BH when p=None.\n- **BH grouping**: Per (alpha, direction, method). E.g., for perm_granger forward at alpha=0.50: 3 seeds x 8 pairs = 24 p-values pooled for BH.\n\n## Per-experiment vs Per-alpha BH FDR Comparison (Forward Only)\n\nFormat: per_exp / per_alpha (out of 8 pairs)\n\n| alpha | exp_name | seed | naive | diff | thres | perm | ty | total |\n|-------|----------|------|-------|------|-------|------|----|-------|\n| 0.00 | a000_s42 | 42 | 8/8 | 4/4 | 3/3 | 0/0 | 1/0 | 16/15 |\n| 0.00 | a000_s43 | 43 | 7/7 | 3/3 | 1/1 | 0/0 | 1/0 | 12/11 |\n| 0.00 | a000_s44 | 44 | 8/8 | 3/3 | 1/1 | 0/0 | 0/0 | 12/12 |\n| 0.10 | a010_s43_bf16 | 43 | 7/7 | 8/8 | 3/3 | 0/0 | 0/0 | 18/18 |\n| 0.25 | a025_s43 | 43 | 8/8 | 3/3 | 0/0 | 0/0 | 0/0 | 11/11 |\n| 0.25 | a025_s44 | 44 | 8/8 | 1/1 | 0/0 | 0/0 | 2/1 | 11/10 |\n| 0.50 | a050_s42 | 42 | 8/8 | 8/8 | 0/0 | 0/0 | 0/0 | 16/16 |\n| 0.50 | a050_s43 | 43 | 8/8 | 6/6 | 0/0 | **6/1** | 0/0 | 20/15 |\n| 0.50 | a050_s44 | 44 | 8/8 | 6/6 | 0/0 | **3/1** | 0/0 | 17/15 |\n| 0.60 | a060_s42_bf16 | 42 | 8/8 | 8/8 | 0/0 | 8/8 | 0/0 | 24/24 |\n| 0.75 | a075_s42 | 42 | 8/8 | 8/8 | 4/4 | 8/8 | 1/0 | 29/28 |\n| 0.75 | a075_s43 | 43 | 8/8 | 8/8 | 0/0 | 5/6 | 1/0 | 22/22 |\n| 0.75 | a075_s44 | 44 | 8/8 | 8/8 | 4/4 | 6/6 | 1/0 | 27/26 |\n| 1.00 | gpt2m_wiki_a100_s42 | 42 | 8/8 | 8/8 | 0/0 | 6/6 | 2/2 | 24/24 |\n| 1.00 | gpt2m_wiki_a100_s43_bf16 | 43 | 8/8 | 7/7 | 8/8 | 1/1 | 0/0 | 24/24 |\n| 1.00 | opt1b_wiki_a100_s42 | 42 | 8/8 | 8/8 | 1/1 | 6/6 | 0/0 | 23/23 |\n| 1.00 | pythia14b_wiki_a100_s42 | 42 | 7/7 | 6/6 | 0/0 | **2/4** | 0/0 | 15/17 |\n| 1.00 | pythia14b_wiki_a100_s43 | 43 | 8/8 | 6/6 | 0/0 | 6/6 | 2/0 | 22/20 |\n| 1.00 | pythia1b_c4_a100_s42 | 42 | 8/8 | 6/6 | 0/0 | 6/6 | 1/0 | 21/20 |\n| 1.00 | pythia1b_c4_a100_s43 | 43 | 8/8 | 6/6 | 0/0 | 4/4 | 2/1 | 20/19 |\n| 1.00 | pythia410m_wiki_a100_s42 | 42 | 8/8 | 8/8 | 0/0 | 6/6 | 2/0 | 24/22 |\n\n## Table 7 (Per-alpha BH FDR): Aggregated Mean Forward Sig/8\n\n| alpha | n_exps | naive | diff | thres | perm | ty |\n|-------|--------|-------|------|-------|------|----|\n| 0.00 | 3 | 7.67 | 3.33 | 1.67 | 0.00 | **0.00** |\n| 0.10 | 1 | 7.00 | 8.00 | 3.00 | 0.00 | 0.00 |\n| 0.25 | 2 | 8.00 | 2.00 | 0.00 | 0.00 | **0.50** |\n| 0.50 | 3 | 8.00 | 6.67 | 0.00 | **0.67** | 0.00 |\n| 0.60 | 1 | 8.00 | 8.00 | 0.00 | 8.00 | 0.00 |\n| 0.75 | 3 | 8.00 | 8.00 | 2.67 | **6.67** | **0.00** |\n| 1.00 | 8 | 7.88 | 6.88 | 1.12 | **4.88** | **0.38** |\n\n### vs Per-experiment BH FDR (delta):\n\n| alpha | naive | diff | thres | perm | ty |\n|-------|-------|------|-------|------|----|\n| 0.00 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **-0.67** |\n| 0.10 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n| 0.25 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **-0.50** |\n| 0.50 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **-2.33** | 0 |\n| 0.60 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n| 0.75 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **+0.33** | **-1.00** |\n| 1.00 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **+0.25** | **-0.75** |\n\n## Key Changes Under Per-alpha BH FDR\n\n### 1. perm_granger at alpha=0.50: Major Drop (-2.33 mean)\n- a050_s43: 6/8 -> 1/8 (delta=-5)\n- a050_s44: 3/8 -> 1/8 (delta=-2)\n- Raw p-values were 0.02-0.06 (borderline). When pooled with a050_s42's non-significant p-values (0.02-0.10), BH thresholds became stricter.\n- Only ece->distinct_1 survives in s43 (p=0.0036, adj_p=0.043) and s44 (p=0.002, adj_p=0.043).\n\n### 2. ty_granger: Consistent Small Drops\n- alpha=0.00: 0.67 -> 0.00 (a000_s42 and a000_s43 each lose 1)\n- alpha=0.25: 1.00 -> 0.50 (a025_s44 loses 1)\n- alpha=0.75: 1.00 -> 0.00 (all 3 seeds lose 1 each)\n- alpha=1.00: 1.12 -> 0.38 (pythia14b_s43 loses 2, pythia1b_c4_s42 loses 1, pythia1b_c4_s43 loses 1, pythia410m_s42 loses 2)\n\n### 3. perm_granger at alpha=0.75 and 1.00: Small Gains\n- alpha=0.75: a075_s43 gains 1 (5->6). Pooling with strongly significant tests from s42/s44 helps borderline cases.\n- alpha=1.00: pythia14b_s42 gains 2 (2->4). Same effect from pooling with 7 other strongly significant experiments.\n\n### 4. Unchanged Methods\n- **naive_xcorr**: No change at any alpha (p-values generally very small).\n- **diff_xcorr**: No change at any alpha.\n- **threshold_onset**: No change (no p-value to correct).\n\n## Impact on Dose-Response Narrative\n\nThe per-alpha BH FDR **strengthens** the dose-response story for perm_granger:\n- alpha=0.50 perm_granger drops from 3.00/8 -> 0.67/8 (much weaker signal)\n- alpha=0.75 perm_granger stays at 6.67/8 (strong signal)\n- The jump from alpha=0.50 to alpha=0.75 becomes even more dramatic (0.67 -> 6.67 vs previous 3.00 -> 6.33), supporting a threshold effect between moderate and high contamination.\n\nThe overall pattern: naive_xcorr and diff_xcorr detect the correlation signal across all alpha levels; perm_granger (causal) shows a clear dose-response that kicks in between alpha=0.50 and alpha=0.60.\n\n## Script Location\n\n- westd-19828: `/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/per_alpha_fdr.py`\n- Full JSON results: `/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/per_alpha_fdr_results.json`\n",

      }
```

````json
{
  "task_type": "analysis:check_westd17855_experiments",
  "project": "distrib_canary_collapse",
  "task_prompt": "## 任务：检查 westd-17855 上 5 个实验的进程状态\n\nwestd-17855 上有 5 个实验在 registry 中标记为 RUNNING，但没有注册 cron 监控。它们提交于约 5-6 小时前，可能已经完成。\n\n### 需要检查的实验\n\n| exp_id | 预期 PID | GPU |\n|--------|---------|-----|\n| qwen35_08b_wiki_a100_s42_v2 | 未知 | cuda:1 |\n| smollm3_3b_wiki_a100_s42_v3 | 未知 | cuda:1 |\n| interv_pred_a075_s42_fp32_v4 | 未知 | cuda:4 |\n| alpha_sweep_a060_s42_fp32_v3 | 未知 | cuda:2 |\n| alpha_sweep_a005_s42_v3 | 未知 | cuda:3 |\n\n### 步骤\n\n1. SSH 到 westd-17855，检查 GPU 使用情况：`nvidia-smi`\n2. 检查是否有 python 训练进程在跑：`ps aux | grep python | grep -v grep`\n3. 检查 tmux sessions：`tmux ls` 和每个 session 的内容\n4. 对于每个实验，检查其输出目录是否存在完成标志：\n   - 基础路径：`/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/`\n   - 查看各实验目录下是否有 `all_metrics.json`（完成标志）\n   - 具体目录名模式：\n     - qwen35: `qwen35_*/` 或 `gen_qwen35_*/`\n     - smollm3: `smollm3_*/` 或 `gen_smollm3_*/`  \n     - interv_pred: `interv_pred_*/`\n     - a060: `a060_*/` 或 `alpha_sweep_a060_*/`\n     - a005: `a005_*/` or `alpha_sweep_a005_*/`\n5. 用 `find /root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/ -name \"all_metrics.json\" -newer /root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/per_alpha_fdr.py -ls` 找近期完成的实验\n6. 检查最近的 nohup/log 输出：`ls -lt /root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/*.log 2>/dev/null | head -10`\n\n### 产出\n\n写一个简洁的状态报告到本地：\n`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/westd17855_status_check.md`\n\n报告格式：\n```\n# westd-17855 实验状态检查 (2026-05-19)\n\n## GPU 状态\n(nvidia-smi 摘要)\n\n## 各实验状态\n| exp_id | 状态 | PID | 完成标志 | 输出目录 | 备注 |\n...\n\n## 需要注意的问题\n(如果有)\n```\n\n对于已完成的实验，额外记录：\n- all_metrics.json 的路径\n- 最终 gen 数（是否跑完 10 代）\n- 是否有错误日志",
````

````json
{
  "task_type": "analysis:batch_3_completed_fp32",
  "project": "distrib_canary_collapse",
  "task_prompt": "## 任务：对 westd-17855 上 3 个已完成的 fp32 实验运行分析\n\n3 个实验刚完成，需要运行 batch_analysis.py 获取 5-method lead-lag 分析结果。\n\n### 已完成实验\n\n| exp_id | 输出目录（westd-17855 上） | alpha |\n|--------|--------------------------|-------|\n| interv_pred_a075_s42_fp32_v4 | /root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/results/intervention/interv_pred_fp32_v4_d080/ | 0.75 (intervention) |\n| alpha_sweep_a060_s42_fp32_v3 | /root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/results/alpha_sweep/a060_s42_fp32_v3_d080/ | 0.60 |\n| alpha_sweep_a005_s42_v3 | /root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/results/alpha_sweep/a005_s42_fp32_v3_d080/ | 0.05 |\n\n### 步骤\n\n1. SSH 到 westd-17855\n2. 确认 all_metrics.json 存在：\n   ```bash\n   for d in results/intervention/interv_pred_fp32_v4_d080 results/alpha_sweep/a060_s42_fp32_v3_d080 results/alpha_sweep/a005_s42_fp32_v3_d080; do\n     ls -la /root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/$d/all_metrics.json\n   done\n   ```\n\n3. 对每个实验运行 batch_analysis.py：\n   ```bash\n   cd /root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse\n   source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base\n   \n   # 分析每个实验\n   python batch_analysis.py --exp_dir results/alpha_sweep/a005_s42_fp32_v3_d080\n   python batch_analysis.py --exp_dir results/alpha_sweep/a060_s42_fp32_v3_d080\n   python batch_analysis.py --exp_dir results/intervention/interv_pred_fp32_v4_d080\n   ```\n   \n   注意：如果 batch_analysis.py 参数不同（如 --input_dir），先查看帮助：\n   ```bash\n   python batch_analysis.py --help\n   ```\n\n4. 检查分析输出。对每个实验，查看：\n   - 5method_leadlag.json（lead-lag 分析结果）\n   - 或类似的分析产出文件\n\n5. 收集关键指标。对每个实验提取：\n   - gen 0-10 的 perplexity, token_entropy, ece, distinct_1/2/3, effective_rank\n   - lead-lag 分析：8 pairs x 5 methods 的显著性（forward direction）\n   - 对于 intervention 实验：intervention 触发 gen 和恢复情况\n\n### 产出\n\n写分析报告到本地文件：\n`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/batch3_fp32_analysis.md`\n\n格式：\n```\n# 三个 fp32 实验分析报告 (2026-05-19)\n\n## 1. alpha_sweep_a005_s42_v3 (α=0.05)\n### 指标轨迹\n(gen-by-gen table)\n### Lead-lag 分析\n(5method results summary)\n### 关键发现\n\n## 2. alpha_sweep_a060_s42_fp32_v3 (α=0.60)\n(同上)\n\n## 3. interv_pred_a075_s42_fp32_v4 (intervention α=0.75)\n(同上)\n\n## 对 Dose-Response Map 的影响\n- α=0.05 填补了哪个格子\n- α=0.60 填补了哪个格子\n- intervention 结果与之前 bf16 版本的对比\n```",
````

_source line 3745 · 2026-05-19T05:21:53.073Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The a025 and a050 experiments don't have explicit precision markers. They predate D079/D080. The key question is: were they run with the D080 dtype bug or not?

D080 bug was: `torch_dtype=None` in iterative_train.py loaded native dtype (fp16 for Pythia-410M). This was fixed with explicit `torch.float32`.

So any experiment before the D080 fix was actually using the model's native precision (fp16 for Pythia-410M). The a025 and a050 experiments predate D080, so they're running in fp16 (not fp32 and not bf16).
 
Now I need to check the timestamps to confirm whether a025 and a050 actually used the pre-D080 code. The D080 fix went in around 2026-05-19 00:22 UTC, and a025 was submitted before that, which means it would have run with the old code and fp16 precision. was about what to put on cuda:2/3/4. Let me think about what's most valuable: I could run α=0.25 fp32 multi-seed experiments (s42/s43/s44) to fill three gap cells, or do the same for α=0.50, but I don't actually have fp32 α=0.50 data yet since those pre-D080 runs were all fp16. The ROADMAP marks α=0.50 as "Boundary ✅" which might indicate it's already complete, but I need to verify what that actually means.

The Director's intent seems clear though: cuda:5 is done with a010_s42_fp32, and now I need to propose what goes on the remaining three cards. The real question is whether to prioritize α=0.25 (the sub-threshold regime where MMCT and PermG diverge) or α=0.50 (the boundary transition point), since both need proper fp32 reruns for D079 compliance.

With three GPUs available, α=0.50 multi-seed fp32 makes more sense because that's where the step function transition actually occurs, and confirming whether the PermG signal there is real or a D080 artifact is crucial. The α=0.25 regime already has solid MMCT evidence and multiple seeds, so it's less urgent. But now I'm wondering whether the existing α=0.25 and α=0.50 data were even affected by D080 in the first place—the bug loaded the model's native float16 dtype, so these experiments may have already been running in float16 rather than fp32.

The real question is whether precision meaningfully changes the lead-lag detection at higher alpha values. From α=0.10, we know fp32 reduces drift by about 2x but doesn't flip the PermG outcome, so the effect might be modest at higher alphas too. Still, running α=0.50 fp32 across three seeds on the available GPUs would give us the boundary behavior we need for the paper, and it's the highest-impact experiment to propose given the time constraints.

Now I'm drafting the Director report and spinning up the MMCT contradiction investigation in parallel.
```

```text
Good. a010_s42_fp32 is now RUNNING in registry with full spec. 

Now I need to report to Director with:
1. Registry updates (3 experiments DONE)
2. a010_s42_fp32 submitted on cuda:5
3. smollm3 status (running on westd-19828, gen 3/10)
4. GPU proposals for cuda:2/3/4
5. Workers running: batch analysis, MMCT contradiction
6. Resource line

For cuda:2/3/4 proposals, I need to think about what's most valuable:

The existing a025 and a050 experiments predate D080. 

D080 established that torch_dtype=None loads native dtype (fp16 for Pythia-410M), while D079 requires fp32 for ≤3B models. The pre-D080 data in the dose-response map might not be valid for the paper's main axis if we're standardizing on fp32, so I need to decide whether to use only fp32-consistent data or keep the existing experiments.

Looking at the roadmap, α=0.25 and α=0.50 are both marked complete with 3 seeds each, but they're likely in fp16. I'm leaning toward running α=0.50 with fp32 across three seeds because the boundary regime is where the PermG step function transitions—that's the critical alpha level for the dose-response story. The D086 FDR analysis showed PermG dropped from 3.00 to 0.67/8 at α=0.50, and fp32 data might shift that result meaningfully. Three seeds give me full cross-seed validation in about 5 hours. each, all complete by ~10:20 UTC — compatible with the Table 7 rewrite timeline

Alternatively, I could propose a mix: 2× α=0.50 fp32 + 1× α=0.25 fp32, but 3 seeds at one alpha level is more methodologically sound.

Actually wait, let me reconsider. If existing a050 data is already fp16 (not the claimed fp32), then ALL the PermG numbers at α=0.50 in the per_alpha_fdr_results.md might be wrong. The per_alpha BH FDR analysis was done on the old data. Rerunning at α=0.50 fp32 would give us clean data.

But is this actually true? Let me think again... The D080 fix was about iterative_train.py. Before the fix, when you passed `torch_dtype=None`, it loaded the model in its native format. For Pythia-410M, that's float16 in HuggingFace. So yes, all pre-D080 Pythia-410M experiments were actually fp16, not fp32 as claimed. This means the lead-lag analysis on those experiments could be affected by precision differences.

Looking at the a010 comparison though: fp32 s43 had PermG=0/8, same as bf16. So precision doesn't matter in the blind zone at α=0.10, but at the boundary with α=0.50, precision effects could diverge. I should write up my recommendation in the Director report.

Before I do that, I need to check whether the existing α=0.00 and α=0.75 experiments are also pre-D080 and might need fp32 reruns too. Let me look at the ROADMAP to see what those experiments actually are. ✅" — these are baseline with no synthetic data, so precision matters less
- α=0.75: "Transitional ✅" — 3 seeds. These ARE important. If they're fp16 too, the PermG 83% might shift.

But the Director already ran a075 fp32 experiments (the interv_pred_v4 was at α=0.75 with intervention, not a pure α=0.75 sweep). Wait, looking at the registry:
- alpha_sweep_a075_s42/s43/s44 — these are the original ones. Are they fp32?

The ROADMAP shows α=0.75 experiments existed before D080 was discovered. So they're fp16 too. But at α=0.75, the PermG signal is very strong (83%), so even with precision effects it might still be robust.

Actually, I'm overthinking this. The Director wants proposals, not a full audit of the existing data. Let me focus on what matters: I should propose running α=0.50 fp32 across three seeds on cuda:2/3/4 since that's the critical boundary where the PermG step function transitions, all current α=0.50 data is fp16 from before the D080 discovery, and three seeds will give me solid cross-seed validation at that key threshold. This should take about five hours per seed and finish well before the other experiments wrap up.

I could also mention α=0.25 as a lower-priority alternative since the signal there comes from MMCT which is less sensitive to precision differences, and flag that α=0.75 might also need rerunning if we want to be thorough, but since we already have a strong signal there it's less urgent.

I'm going to keep my proposal focused and let the Director decide what makes sense.
```
